> * An **API/controller** clause is verified against that component's image.
> * **Never cite an image sha as a row's evidence.** The deploy treadmill rolls
>   roughly every 30 minutes; a sha is stale before the next reader arrives.
>   Cite what you observed on the rendered surface.

## Status icons — the canonical definitions

These six are the ENTIRE vocabulary. `scripts/uat-audit.py` fails the build on any
verdict glyph outside this set, because an uncounted glyph is silently dropped by
`uat-backfill.py` and then reads downstream as "never walked" — which turns an
unreadable verdict into a missing pass. Four rows carried a stray `◑` that way, and
`⏳` dropped thirteen more, before the guard existed.

| icon | name | means | what moves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `✅` | ⏳ | Every clause in the test case was measured on the CURRENT env and held, with a control showing the check could have come out the other way. | Nothing — until the env is replaced or the clause changes, when it returns to ☐. |
| `❌` | FAIL | Measured on the current env and it did NOT hold. A named, reproducible defect. This is the honest home for anything undelivered — a feature that has not shipped is a FAIL, not a block. | A fix, deployed, then re-walked. |
| `⚠️` | PARTIAL | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict.** Some clauses proven, others not. A test case whose clause does not fully hold is a FAILING test case, so a row may not be REPORTED in this state: resolve it to ✅ or ❌ before a convergence cycle is captured. It exists so a walk-in-progress can be saved mid-flight. | Closing the named residual (→ ✅) or accepting it as the failure it is (→ ❌). |
| `⛔` | SUPERSEDED | The test case cannot be answered as written — its subject no longer exists, or it asserts a value the schema forbids. **Not** a parking space for hard work. | Retire-and-REPLACE, one-for-one, recorded in `uat-retirements.csv`. |
| `☐` | NOTRUN | Never walked on this env. Includes a freshly replaced clause, which must NOT inherit the retired row's verdict. | Walking it. |
| `⏳` | PENDING — CARRIED, AND OWED A WALK | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict**, same rule as PARTIAL. Written by `scripts/reset-uat.py` when the env is replaced AND `scripts/uat-confidence.py` no longer stands behind the row here — it is due, or its box is 0, or the scheduler has never scored it. Distinct from never-walked (`☐`), and distinct from a carried row that KEEPS its `✅`: a wipe is not a failure, so a verdict the scheduler still holds is not demoted at all (founder 2026-08-13 — *"wiping and recreating an environment doesn't necessarily mean wiping test results as well"*). **Nothing is ever deleted in either case.** The Evidence cell keeps the original stamp verbatim, env label and artifact link included, and `docs/ledger/uat-observations.csv` is untouched. A row may not be REPORTED in this state. | Re-walking on the live env — `uat-confidence.py --due` says which rows this cycle owes — then ✅ or ❌. |

**The denominator is STONE at 286.** Retiring a row always carries a replacement so
the count never moves; `scripts/uat-snapshot.py` refuses any other total. A
floating denominator makes every percentage incomparable across cycles, and it has
drifted before.

**Evidence is per-env, but a wipe is not a failure.** A ✅ answers one question —
does this hold on the machine in front of us — and re-provisioning replaces the
machine, not the code. So a verdict proven on a predecessor is CARRIED, and
`scripts/uat-confidence.py` decides for how long: a Beta-Bernoulli confidence over
time-discounted, environment-tagged evidence, with a Leitner box that sets the
re-walk interval. `scripts/uat-drift-guard.py` asks that scheduler, per row:

* the scheduler still stands behind the row → the `✅` **stands**, predecessor
  stamp and all. Nothing is demoted, nothing is deleted, the row keeps counting.
* the row is due here, its box is 0, or it was never scored → the `✅` is
  **refused** and the row wears `⏳` until it is walked on this env.

Carry-forward is a grace period, not an amnesty: decay pulls every quiet row back
to due-ness, and then a predecessor's screenshot stops carrying it. A drop across
an env boundary is the ledger working; a drop WITHIN one env is a regression.

That is NOT a flush, and it is not a blanket demotion either. Both errors have now
been made. The rule was once written "each new env flushes ALL prior evidence", and
under it the score reset to near-zero on every fresh prov (measured: 133 green
2026-07-30 → 25 on 2026-08-03) — the treadmill `uat-confidence.py` exists to end;
founder 2026-08-11 superseded it: *"That is the whole point not to reset the
fucking success results to zero!!!"* Then on 2026-08-13 the hw295 → hw296 rotation
put `main` red on 47 rows and PR #6250 quieted the guard by demoting all 47 to
`⏳`, dropping the headline 225 → 178. No row was measured and no evidence was
lost — but the rows stopped COUNTING, which is the outcome carry-forward exists to
prevent. Founder, same day: *"wiping and recreating an environment doesn't
necessarily mean wiping test results as well — this is why we created the new
framework."* **Necessarily** is the design: the scheduler decides which, and in
#6258 39 of those 47 were restored to `✅` with their evidence cells untouched,
while the 8 it does owe a walk (W3 · 55 · 57 · 62 · 67 · 69 · 71 · 188) stayed
`⏳`. That
restoration was a reinstatement of prior measurement, not a new walk — no row
below was re-observed to produce it.

**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

So the sweep positively EVALUATED this live Sovereign's infrastructure in both regions, 65 times, and protected it each time. `reaped=0` on all 65 passes. 🛑 WHY THIS IS NOT A VACUOUS PASS — the control is built into the same log: the sweep is DISCRIMINATING, not inert. In the very passes that skip hw295 it identifies 62 genuinely-orphaned OBS buckets left behind by long-since-wiped predecessor environments and marks each `would-reap`. (Their names are deliberately not quoted here: a ✅ row must not carry a predecessor env token, and `uat-drift-guard.py` refused this cell until they were removed — correctly, since that is exactly how stale evidence hides in a green row.) A guard that protected everything would be indistinguishable from a sweep that does nothing; this one separates the live deployment from 62 dead ones and gets both right. SOURCE THIS CONFIRMS: `janitor.go:764-768` — `case "wiped":` is the ONLY eligible branch, `default:` protects. A future-added status therefore fails SAFE, and `failed` is protected too (#4454).
| R2 | network | [#4467](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/4467) | Cross-node pod TCP carries full-size data packets without DF-drop in BOTH regions — the Cilium datapath MTU is internally consistent (node 1500 / pod+cilium_host 1420 / cilium_wg0 1340 under routing-mode=native since #4656; the pre-#4656 wording pinned VXLAN + MTU 1370, a config this platform no longer runs). | hw295-2026-08-13 | ✅ | hw295-2026-08-13T02:2xZ LIVE DATAPATH MEASUREMENT, BOTH REGIONS, WITH A FAILING CONTROL. ✅ — this row asks for two different things and the earlier ☐ was correct to separate them: a CONFIG read is not a DATAPATH proof. Both are now done. CONFIG, read per-NODE (not from the ConfigMap) via the hostNetwork cilium agent, and IDENTICAL in both regions: eth0=**1500**, cilium_host=**1420**, cilium_net=**1420**, cilium_wg0=**1340** — exactly the three figures this clause names for routing-mode=native since #4656. DATAPATH, measured with DF actually set: from a pod, a UDP socket with `IP_MTU_DISCOVER=IP_PMTUDISC_DO` connected to a pod on a DIFFERENT node reports pathMTU=**1340**, and a send of exactly pathMTU-28 = 1312 bytes SUCCEEDS. Measured cross-node inside region A (10.42.1.150, 10.42.3.86), cross-node inside region B (10.43.1.30, 10.43.4.154), and CROSS-REGION in both directions (A->10.43.1.30, B->10.42.1.150). Every path reports the same 1340 — no black-holed or silently-reduced PMTU anywhere. THE CONTROL, which is what makes the above mean anything: one byte more (1313) is REFUSED with `EMSGSIZE / Message too long` on every single path. The probe can fail, and does, at exactly the predicted boundary. WHY 1340 AND NOT 1420 — internally consistent, not a discrepancy: `enable-wireguard=true`, so all pod-to-pod traffic is encrypted through cilium_wg0, whose MTU IS 1340. 1420 is the unencrypted pod/cilium_host figure and 1500 the node figure; the effective pod path MTU is correctly the wg0 one. DELIVERY, so this does not rest on a send() return alone: the DF probe proves the local stack's PMTU and DF handling, while the CNPG WAL stream proves bytes actually arrive — every standby reads `state=streaming` at a matching LSN across nodes AND across regions, which is sustained bulk TCP over exactly these paths. |

Region B's Postgres never had those roles created with that password. Timing corroborates: region-B app secrets are 10m old against region A's 108m, matching the fresh keycloak-0 (9m36s) and the second harbor-core ReplicaSet (9m42s) — the resync changed nothing. SECONDARY FINDING worth a guard: HR-Ready is NOT steady-state — `bp-harbor` reports Ready=True `Helm install succeeded` in region B while harbor-core and harbor-jobservice CrashLoop, so any check reading HelmRelease Ready as a convergence proxy passes here. PARTITION: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. Region B's Postgres never had the `keycloak`/`harbor` roles created with the synced password, so re-syncing the secret is provably a no-op; either the region-B roles must be provisioned or region-B apps must resolve to the region-A primary. A re-walk cannot change this and no merged fix is waiting on a roll. |

The delivery chain behind it, each link observed: OpenBao `secret/catalyst/anthropic/token` written by the seed reconciler → ExternalSecret `walkthree/agenity-anthropic-token` flipped `SecretSyncedError` → `SecretSynced "Secret was synced"` → target Secret carries `anthropicApiKey` + `credentialsJson`. NOTE the ESO `refreshInterval` is `1h`, so the sync needed a `force-sync` annotation to land inside the walk; without it the credential is correct but up to an hour late — worth a shorter interval on a credential whose accessToken lives ~5h. THE CREDENTIAL CHAIN IS NOW CLOSED AT BOTH ENDS. #6264's mothership producer `Handler.seedSovereignAnthropicCredentials` had never executed: the mothership catalyst-api ran `4ebf34f` and `git merge-base --is-ancestor 7c8466771 4ebf34fd0` returned NO. BOTH were closed this session: the mothership Secret now carries `anthropic-api-key` (108 B) + `anthropic-credentials-json` (509 B) with source-wins `catalyst/sovereign-anthropic-credentials` (`apiKey`/`credentialsJson`, same bytes) so a helm reconcile re-projects rather than reverts, and catalyst-api was rolled to `c4d3c95` — the tag git main itself declares (`api-deployment-kustomize.yaml:189`) and which DOES carry the producer (`merge-base --is-ancestor 7c8466771 c4d3c95` → 0). Env materialised in the running pod: `API_KEY_LEN=108 CREDS_JSON_LEN=509`. The credential authenticates for real, not by inspection: `POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` with `authorization: Bearer` + `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20` returned **HTTP 200** with body `ALIVE`, against a same-bytes wrong-scheme CONTROL (`x-api-key`) returning **401 authentication_error** — so the 200 measures the credential, not the endpoint. PRODUCER EXECUTION PROVEN, not inferred from image contents: hw296's own catalyst-api (`c4d3c95`) `[SEED-RECONCILE]` pass at 00:04:16Z logged `anthropic seed: wrote OpenBao path so every Org's agenity ExternalSecret resolves` with `credentialClass=valid`, `detail=claudeAiOauth accessToken valid ~4h longer`, `apiKeyBytes=108`, `credentialsJsonBytes=509`, immediately followed by `[SEED-RECONCILE] self-heal complete — path verified non-empty by READ-BACK`. The three preceding cycles (23:44:16Z, 23:54:16Z, and 00:04:16Z's own opening line) logged the `🛑 anthropic seed SKIPPED` / `self-heal did NOT take` pair, so the flip is observed against its own before-state on the same reconciler. 🛑 SCOPE OF THE MOTHERSHIP FIX, STATED HONESTLY: `seedSovereignAnthropicCredentials` is called from ONE place — `PutKubeconfig` (`kubeconfig.go:716`), the cloud-init postback — so it is a PROVISION-TIME hop. hw296 posted its kubeconfig at 16:19Z on 2026-08-13, long before the producer existed, and NOTHING re-runs that hop for an already-provisioned Sovereign. The mothership fix therefore seeds every Sovereign provisioned FROM NOW ON and did not, and could not, retroactively seed hw296. hw296 was closed by writing `catalyst-system/sovereign-anthropic-credentials` on hw296 directly (the operator-rotatable seam the #6163/#6215 live read was built for) — declared here as the deliberate env-local unblock it is, not as the durable fix. The remaining durable gap, named: no component re-seeds an EXISTING Sovereign from the mothership; the Sovereign-side reconciler self-heals only from the Sovereign's OWN Secret. |

Traced `products/catalyst/bootstrap/ui/src/entities/deployment/store.ts` (957 lines), the wizard's state model: (1) NO FABRICATED DEFAULTS — initial state is `regionProviders: {}` and `regionCloudRegions: {}`, empty maps, so nothing is pre-populated for the User to unknowingly accept. (2) VALUES ARE SET, NEVER DERIVED — the only writers are the explicit user actions `setRegionProvider` / `setRegionCloudRegion` / `applyProviderToAll`; there is no inference from FQDN, no fallback constant, no copy from a sibling region. (3) CROSS-CONTAMINATION IS ACTIVELY PREVENTED — `setRegionProvider` (store.ts:355-356) writes the new provider AND simultaneously clears that region's cloudRegion to `''`, so a region name chosen under a previous provider cannot survive a provider switch. (4) TOPOLOGY CHANGE RESETS THE LOT — `setTopology` zeroes regionProviders, regionCloudRegions, the per-region CP/worker sizes, the worker counts, and the provider tokens/validation, and the in-code comment names precisely the fabrication this defends against: "a stale SKU at index 3 carried over from CITADEL into a SOLO would silently feed the cost rollup an unreachable region". A wizard that fabricated would show a default provider on first paint or carry a region across a topology change; this one shows neither. |

CAVEAT RECORDED so the next walker does not repeat my error: a cookie-only probe returns **401** on all three, because this console authenticates by OIDC access token in sessionStorage rather than by the session cookie. Reading that 401 alone would have produced a false "renders authenticated UI with a dead session" defect; the Bearer control refutes it. |

Verified on the SERVED artifact, not source: `GET /api/v1/catalog` (81 entries) returns `modes: [single-region]` for bp-seaweedfs, bp-alloy, bp-trivy and bp-openova-mcp alike — and bp-alloy's repo blueprint.yaml carries NO `placementSchema` at all, so #6282's second leg (the permissive `AllowedByBlueprint` branch for the 28 Blueprints with no `modes[]`) is UNREACHABLE on this Sovereign: ZERO of the 81 live entries has an absent or empty `modes[]`. 71 are `[single-region]` only; the 10 that name a DR posture (bp-cnpg-pair, bp-continuum, bp-gitea, bp-guacamole, bp-harbor, bp-keycloak, bp-newapi, bp-openbao, bp-postgres, bp-wordpress-tenant) ALL carry a perTopology entry for it WITH a switchover mechanism, so gate 3 never fires either. AND THE READY PATH IS CLOSED TOO: `topologyOverrideFromPlacement` (topology_override.go:74-88) returns the Blueprint's own spelling whenever the declared mode appears in `topology.supported[]`, so any DR declaration a Blueprint supports RESOLVES to that DR variant and `buildContinuumPlan` mints `dr-<app>`. The single live Blueprint naming a DR mode absent from `supported[]` is bp-postgres (active-passive), and its `defaults.multi-region` is active-hot-standby, so it mints one as well. | 164 | cutover | [#3379](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3379) | The cutover group status reflects its real children (a group with a failed child reads failed, not a fake Succeeded). | hw296-2026-08-14 | ✅ | hw296-2026-08-14T00:00Z ✅ VERIFIED on a REAL failed child, not a contrived one. During the operator-initiated cutover attempt fired at 2026-08-13T23:56:14Z, step-03 harbor-prewarm genuinely failed (#6280, the missing namespaces RBAC grant), giving the group a real failed child alongside two real succeeded ones. `GET /api/v1/deployments/e689e3b34a75fdec/jobs` returned, on the wire: `cutover type=group status=failed children=11`, with `cutover-step-gitea-mirror status=succeeded`, `cutover-step-harbor-projects status=succeeded`, `cutover-step-harbor-prewarm status=failed`. The dedicated group endpoint agrees: `GET .../jobs/cutover` → `jobName=cutover type=group kind=group status=failed children=11`. So a group holding two succeeded children and one failed child reads FAILED, never a rolled-up Succeeded — which is exactly the fake-green this row exists to refute. The rule is not incidental: products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/jobs/store.go:815-824 makes hasFailed win the precedence switch over running/pending, store.go:765-772 renders an EMPTY group pending rather than succeeded, and store.go:777-801 additionally counts a health-axis degraded/failing leaf as a failure so an unhealthy reconciler can never be averaged into a green rollup. Regression coverage: internal/jobs/activity_bridge_test.go:165 "Step 2 fails — the group MUST roll up to failed". Note for future walkers: the /jobs TABLE hides type=group rows by design (JobsTable.tsx:396-402), so this row is evidenced on the wire and on the group's JobDetail, not as a table row. |

(2) The payload's top key is `jobs`, not `items`; a selector that only reads `items` yields an empty list and a 400 `missing-path-params` that looks like an API fault but is an empty argument. NOT A GAP: the #4000 self-heal path (`sovereign_secondary_kubeconfig_selfheal.go`) never fired, and should not have — it exists only for envs where no `.nodeip` was ever supplied AND the host is unreachable. WHAT THE RIGHT DOOR DELIVERS, measured: HTTPRoute g7freea/bp-openclaw-controller with hostname **openclaw.g7freea.omani.homes** parented to cilium-gateway-console, resolving in DNS — the route the ❌ cell recorded as matching nothing anywhere on the Sovereign; and OIDC_ISSUER_URL **https://keycloak.g7freea.omani.homes/realms/org-g7freea** with a matching per-Org keycloak HTTPRoute — a real tenant realm, not the `https://keycloak.example.local/realms/example` placeholder that made the ❌ cell's install 503 by construction. THE THREE PASS CLAUSES, EACH MEASURED AND EACH FAILING: bp-openclaw HelmRelease **Ready=False** — `Helm install failed for release g7freea/bp-openclaw with chart bp-openclaw@0.2.17: context deadline exceeded`; controller pod **0 replicas, never created** — the ReplicaSet reports FailedCreate `exceeded quota: plan-quota, requested limits.cpu=250m, used 2550m, limited 2`; and `curl https://openclaw.g7freea.omani.homes/readyz` → **503 no healthy upstream**. ROOT CAUSE: the plan-s ResourceQuota is limits.cpu=2 / limits.memory=4Gi, and the per-Org default app set (agenity, keycloak, keycloak-postgresql, newapi, stalwart-tenant, wordpress-tenant, openclaw) is ALREADY at 2550m / 4672Mi — over the hard cap before openclaw is considered. The Sovereign renders an Org whose bundled apps cannot fit the plan it was created on. CONTROLS, same gateway and same Org, so a 503 cannot be read as a gateway or region artifact: agenity.g7freea.omani.homes answers **302** from a 1/1 Running pod on that same console gateway; wordpress.g7freea.omani.homes answers 503, the same quota-starved class. SECOND CONTROL that isolates quota from readiness logic: g7doora carries NO ResourceQuota (its create failed before the boundary landed), so its openclaw controller pod IS created and runs — `openclaw-controller listening on :8080; issuer=... authRequired=true` with `idle reaper started: timeout=30m0s sweepInterval=7m30s` and NO `list pods … context deadline exceeded` loop — and it is still **0/1** because the kubelet reports `Readiness probe failed: HTTP probe failed with statuscode: 503`: readyzHandler (platform/openclaw/controller/main.go:256) 503s on its OIDC JWKS leg because that Org's issuer host is NXDOMAIN. So of the four layers this row names: controller image RESOLVES (openclaw-controller:5f0ef22 pulled and running), reaper apiserver egress RESOLVES, TLS/route and OIDC are now SUPPLIED by the overlay, and readiness fails on capacity first and on issuer reachability second. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE — the per-Org plan quota must accommodate the app set the same plan installs, or the overlay must not emit apps the plan cannot host; openclaw's own chain is otherwise wired correctly by the right door. |
